# JouleWise Task Queue

This is the live queue for JouleWise work. When the user gives a new task, first
triage it here instead of assuming it should happen immediately.

## Intake Rule For New Tasks

For every new user task:

1. Read `RUN_STATE.md`.
2. Read this file.
3. Check `git status --short --branch`.
4. Review the last 2-3 commits with `git log --oneline --decorate -3`.
5. Check relevant handoffs in `docs/run_reports/`.
6. If `RUN_STATE.md` contains an ACTIVE `ACTIVE_STOP_CARD`, that card
   outranks this queue. Execute or preserve the card's resume/cleanup
   instructions before considering any lower-ranked work.
7. Decide whether the task is:
   - urgent workspace hygiene,
   - Phase 1 evidence work,
   - Phase 2 implementation prep,
   - later-phase research work,
   - documentation/reporting,
   - or unrelated/new scope.
8. Place or update the task in the queue with priority, rationale, evidence,
   and blockers.
9. If executing it now, say why it outranks the current top task.
10. Closure rule (D-023): a row may move to Completed only after the
    corresponding phase exit-checklist matrix row already shows the same
    status with dated evidence, and the Completed row's evidence cell
    must cite that matrix row (file + item id). If no matrix row exists
    for the work, say so explicitly in the evidence cell.

## Priority Scale

- **P0 Safety**: prevents accidental data loss, bad commits, broken handoffs, or
  corrupted repo state.
- **P1 Phase Gate**: required to close the current phase or unblock the next
  phase responsibly.
- **P2 Next Slice**: next implementation slice after current phase gates are
  adequately planned or closed.
- **P3 Research Expansion**: useful experiment or feature, but not needed for
  current gate.
- **P4 Polish**: quality-of-life, dashboard polish, formatting, cleanup, or
  presentation work.

## Ranking Factors

Rank higher when a task:

- Prevents accidental loss or bad Git history.
- Produces evidence for the current phase exit checklist.
- Removes ambiguity for multiple later steps.
- Is required before physical hardware time is spent.
- Is cheap to verify and reduces future confusion.
- Matches the current phase better than jumping ahead.

Rank lower when a task:

- Depends on unavailable hardware or supervisor input.
- Is a later-phase feature.
- Adds polish before a runnable vertical slice exists.
- Produces code without a clear run-bundle or test artifact.

## Ready/Shelf Rule

A partially built or proposed task is **READY** only when it has:

- one authority document or stream-log pointer,
- bounded files/modules or a bounded artifact target,
- explicit acceptance evidence or a verification command,
- no hidden hardware/user/token-budget dependency, and
- a named lane (`[AGENT]`, `[QUIET-MAC]`, or `[ED-EXTERNAL]`).

If any of those are missing, keep the item as a shelved concept or
planning note instead of letting it compete with executable queue work.
Half-finished work should be resumed only through its authority pointer
and stop-card/checkpoint state, not by inference from prose summaries.

## Machine-State Lanes (adopted C-007, 2026-07-07)

Every task carries a lane; a session picks the top task COMPATIBLE with
its machine state, not the top task absolutely:

- **[QUIET-MAC]** — measurement campaigns only: no agent fleet, no Codex
  load, idle gate will flag contamination.
- **[AGENT]** — code, docs, feasibility spikes; safe during agent-heavy
  sessions.
- **[ED-EXTERNAL]** — needs the user: advisor, calendar, device access,
  purchases, destinations.

| FROZEN-RECEIPT-CONSTANT-STALE-01 | P2 Next Slice | 2026-08-27 | Stop successor-family PACK_AUTHENTICATION from resting on `CURRENT_FROZEN_RECEIPT_SHA256`, the generator constant that names the PREDECESSOR's freeze receipt | No phase exit-checklist matrix row exists. Landed on PR #214 (branch `fix/packauth-soundness-rows`; gauntlet: design consult → implementation → independent audit → fix round → delta re-audit). The staleness is structural, not an accident: `embedded_generator_bytes()` emits a successor generator by substituting only `CURRENT_FAMILY_SUFFIX`, so every successor family is born one generation behind and `_v4` — emitted from the `_v3` generators — will name the `_v2` freeze-0002 digest too. Executed across all nine packs (`docs/process_traces/2026-08-26-t26-s3/characterization.md`, replayable via `replay-characterization.sh`): the three ordinal-1 packs match their own receipt and pass a bare `--check`; all six successors exit 1 with `generation failed: the current frozen identity requires preserve mode`. ADOPTED RULING: the constant is NOT refreshed per generation and the authentication path stops depending on it. Refreshing is impossible rather than merely undesirable — a successor generator is emitted before its own freeze receipt exists, so the digest it would need does not yet exist, and editing the generator after the mint would change already-frozen pack bytes (D-153 would then force a new family generation). The ruling is recorded normatively in `docs/contracts/receipt_histsem_verifier.md` §'Adopted A93 ruling' (dated 2026-08-26) and pinned by `test_frozen_receipt_constant_variants_do_not_change_the_authentication_verdict`, which holds one derivation fixed, varies the constant through absent / matches-current / names-predecessor / unrelated / computed / duplicated / malformed forms and requires an IDENTICAL authentication verdict with only diagnostic differences. The constant is now read by a TOTAL `ast.parse`/`ast.literal_eval` extractor that never raises and never reaches the verdict; its relation is recorded with `authentication_dependency: false`. An earlier shape that refused a flagless generator merely because the constant existed was caught by the independent audit (F1, blocker) and removed — preserve CAPABILITY, not the constant, now selects the invocation. NOT done here: PACKAUTH-PRESERVE-TAUTOLOGY-01, which shares the branch and stays open on the delta re-audit's D1 blocker. A binding `docs/decision_log.md` entry for this ruling is OWED at the merge gate (magistrate's act). |

| MLX-ACID-SIGABRT-01 | P2 Next Slice | 2026-08-27 | Cure the pre-existing pytest SIGABRT at `joulewise/adapters/mlx_runtime.py:1159` reached via the four ACID tests in `tests/test_arm_readiness_evidence_t0.py`, so the suite is pytest-collectable without deselection | No phase exit-checklist matrix row exists. Landed on PR #203 (branch `fix/ci-reliability-batch`; three rounds: the cure, the audit fix round, and the delta re-audit round that is the branch head). The abort is a nanobind double-registration: `mlx.core` is a C-extension whose native initializer calls `abort()` if it runs twice, and the hidden diagnostic is `Critical nanobind error: refusing to add duplicate key "cpu" to enumeration "mlx.core.DeviceType"!`. It ran twice because `author_environment` injected its synthetic `mlx_lm` with `mock.patch.dict(sys.modules, ...)`, whose exit does `clear(); update(<snapshot taken on entry>)` — `mlx`/`mlx.core` are imported LAZILY inside that context by `_mlx_metal_memory`, so they were absent from the snapshot, were evicted on exit, and the next test's `import_module` re-executed the loaded shared object. Cured code-side on both halves per the row's fence (a process-level abort cannot be contained by a marker): `_resolve_mlx_core()` keeps a reference to the loaded extension, and `author_environment` patches the single `mlx_lm` key. The resolver took THREE audit rounds to get right, and the final rule is the record: `sys.modules` is authoritative while it holds an entry; a `None` entry is Python's blocked-import sentinel and still raises `mlx_core_not_found`; membership in the cache turns on what a module IS (`ExtensionFileLoader`) and not on who imported it — the first shape cached whatever sat in `sys.modules` (test stand-ins would have outlived their own `finally` blocks and fed real evidence), the second cached only self-imports (missing the real extension that `mlx_lm.load()` imports before the snapshot, leaving the abort reachable by another route); and the resolver RESTORES the evicted key rather than stepping around it, because while the key is missing any importer in the process — not just this adapter — re-runs the initializer. Seven defect-shaped regressions, each red-before/green-after: `test_mlx_metal_memory_reuses_cached_core_after_module_eviction`, `test_author_environment_preserves_modules_imported_inside_context`, and the five `MlxCoreResolutionTests` (`..._standin_never_enters_the_extension_cache`, `..._blocked_import_sentinel_is_honoured_over_the_cache`, `..._extension_imported_by_another_component_is_remembered`, `..._resolving_after_an_eviction_repairs_sys_modules`, `..._a_standin_left_in_the_cache_is_discarded_not_served`). Acceptance: pre-fix `pytest -k acid` exit 134 with `Fatal Python error: Aborted`; post-fix, same command with the skips temporarily removed, exit 1 `3 failed, 1 skipped, 32 deselected` and a normal summary; the four ACID skips now read `STRUCTURAL-BLOCKED: fixture R1 schemas require FIXTURE-MODERNIZATION-01 (A84)` (the canonical census prefix — the first attempt wrote `STRUCTURE-BLOCKED:` and broke `tests/test_s0_blocked_enumeration.py` with `AssertionError: 0 != 4`, caught by audit and independently by CI on the pre-fix head; the census moves 17/4 to 21/0) and stay skipped, because this row was NEVER sufficient for them — A84 is; `pytest --collect-only -q` reports 4008 tests collected with zero deselection. NOT done here: A84 itself. RESIDUAL RISK recorded, not fixed: the reference lives in the adapter module global, so an `importlib.reload` of the adapter would reset it; no reloader exists anywhere in `joulewise/`, `scripts/` or `tests/`, and the key-restoration above makes the state self-repairing on the next probe. Custody: `docs/process_traces/2026-08-26-t26-ci-reliability/`. |
| CALEXITS-FOURTH-SHAPE-01 | P2 Next Slice | 2026-08-27 | Complete the mutation-race terminal-shape taxonomy after run 32739939880 failed `test_forced_auto_maintenance_mutation_reproduces_cleanup_race` with a fourth shape the widened predicate rejects | No phase exit-checklist matrix row exists. Closed on verification via PR #203; the CURE had already landed at `ddb1f633` on 2026-08-24, after this row's text was authored, and the row simply predated it. The preserved attempt-1 log (custodied at `docs/process_traces/2026-08-26-t26-ci-reliability/evidence/calexits-fourth-shape-run32739939880-attempt1-py3.11.txt`, from an archive with sha256 `f60638ebcea81bf5584a722604760a6e617ff6df70c1251222e0e2c486d8108c`) shows `RACE_EXERCISED=0 NO_RACE_PRE_WRITE=0 TRACE_INCOMPLETE=0` — every counter zero, because the test died on its first attempt at `self.assertTrue(pack_evidence.complete, ...)` before classifying anything — and an event tail in which the detached `git maintenance` process enters `loose-objects`, spawns only `git prune-packed --quiet` (exit 0) and exits 0. No `git pack-objects` child was ever spawned: the absent-pack-child shape. The 3.14 job of the same run passed with `RACE_EXERCISED=1`, which is why the shape read as intermittent rather than as a broken predicate. `ddb1f633` covers exactly that — `_classify_pack_cleanup` branches on `evidence.pack_child_absent` FIRST and returns `NO_PACK_CHILD`, the mutation test guards its completeness assertion with `if not pack_evidence.pack_child_absent:`, and a raised cleanup errno still outranks the absent child so it cannot mask a real shape-A race. Each of the five modelled shapes A–E carries a deterministic `_synthetic_pack_topology` regression, and that builder raises `"synthetic topology terminal shapes are mutually exclusive"` if asked for two at once. Stated precisely, because an audit round caught the overclaim: that proves MUTUAL EXCLUSION among the modelled shapes, NOT that the taxonomy is exhaustive — a sixth topology would classify `TRACE_INCOMPLETE` and fail loudly rather than pass silently, which is the property soundness needs. Acceptance evidence: bench, the full `tests/test_calibration_exits.py` module passes `46 passed, 208 subtests passed`; hosted, of the 36 `main` runs whose head descends from both this cure and `a28b55bf`, 31 are green, and none of the 5 failures is this test (four are `test_gen_state` live-ID oracle drift during 2026-08-26/27 bookkeeping; the fifth, run 32813091203, is the identity-pins teardown race cured on the same PR). |
| PLANTEST-RGLOB-RACE-01 | P2 Next Slice | 2026-08-27 | Cure the `checkout_inventory` rglob/scandir race in `tests/test_d117_decode_contrast_plan.py` (run 32745254371: `os.scandir` died mid-walk when a directory vanished during the recursive inventory) | No phase exit-checklist matrix row exists. Closed on verification via PR #203; the CURE had already landed at `a28b55bf` on 2026-08-24, after this row's text was authored. `checkout_inventory` no longer uses `Path.rglob`: it walks with `os.walk(topdown=True, onerror=<re-raise>)` and prunes `.git` and `__pycache__` from `dirnames` before descending, so the git loose-object fan-out directory that vanished under a background repack is never scandir-ed at all. Exactness is deliberately preserved rather than traded for tolerance — a real, non-excluded directory vanishing mid-walk still raises, and the regression says so in as many words. Three deterministic regressions ship with it, driven by `_VanishingDirectoryHarness`, which patches `os.scandir` to pause the walking thread at a chosen directory while a worker thread `rmtree`s it: `test_git_object_store_is_never_walked_and_cannot_toll_the_inventory` (cites the hosted provenance verbatim and asserts the hook never even fires — not firing IS the green result), `test_inventory_raises_when_a_real_directory_vanishes_mid_walk`, and `test_inventory_matches_the_rglob_semantics_it_replaces` (symlinks, dangling links, empty directories). Acceptance evidence: bench, `tests/test_d117_decode_contrast_plan.py` three consecutive runs at `24 passed, 1 skipped, 24 subtests passed` each; hosted, the same 36-run/31-green census recorded on CALEXITS-FOURTH-SHAPE-01 above, in which this race did not recur. Same failure family as the identity-pins teardown race cured on PR #203 and as `EVIDENCE-AUTHOR-GIT-TEARDOWN-01`: a background git process mutating a fixture tree another thread is walking or removing. |
| CODEX-BRIDGE-SANDBOX-01 | P2 Next Slice | 2026-08-25 | Correct `scripts/codex-bridge` review-mode sandbox enforcement so the launched sandbox is the one the audit manifest records | Merged as PR #191 (`9fd185ac`). All three acceptance bullets met. The 2026-08-05 defect was real and verbatim: the non-app `review` launch carried no `-s` flag at all while every observer row recorded `observer_sandbox=read-only`, and `new` spelled `-s workspace-write` as a literal at the launch site with the recorded value in a separate variable — the exact arrangement that lets the two drift. Cure: `observer_sandbox` becomes the only place a mode is named, feeding `sandbox_args=(-s "$observer_sandbox")` into every non-app launch (`new`, `resume`, `review`); `resume` was included deliberately rather than shipping a manifest `sandbox` field that would misstate it. Flag position verified against the installed CLI (`-s` is an option of `codex exec`, not of its `review`/`resume` subcommands). Evidence is three-layered: **argv capture** (the fake-`codex` harness records exactly one sandbox flag per mode, the expected value, positioned before the subcommand word), **records** (the new `invocation_manifest.jsonl` `sandbox` field and every observer row equal the captured value), and a **source-level guard** (no launch site names a mode literally; `sandbox_args=(-s "$observer_sandbox")` is present) — the "cannot drift apart" clause made mechanical. Defect-shaped proof: with the script reverted to `733914e1` the four new tests fail 3F/1E and review's captured argv comes back with no `-s`; restored, `Ran 9 tests ... OK`. Test lines: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_codex_bridge_observer` → 9 OK; `bash -n scripts/codex-bridge` clean; `compileall` clean. NOT done here, by the PR's own record: the kernel row was fenced off from that session (closed in this wave instead); `scripts/test_timings.json` left untouched so its harvested-maximum provenance stays true; and *that the flag takes effect* rests on the same argument the bridge already relies on for `-m`/`-c` in review mode rather than on a live quota-spending review run. |

## WO-MINT-ESTIMATOR-VOCAB — COMPLETED (`e11b1ad`, 2026-08-12; registered 2026-08-11)

Status: **COMPLETED** in `e11b1ad44d58eef3a5eb56b8e878d82345b531ba`
(#140) and recorded in the Completed table above. The section below is the
preserved registration/design record, not live work.

Historical registration priority: P2 (rises to P0 critical-path ONLY if Ed rules the gamma-arm
claim capability must ship in the main paper this cycle — D-133 flagged
schedule call). The pinned mint scripts (mint_floor_artifact.py,
mint_floor_artifact_generalized.py) carry ZERO estimator vocabulary: the
v2 mint recomputes comparative floors with the default core under an
exact-equality check, so a common-mode-estimator cell CANNOT be minted
today. Scope: add governed estimator vocabulary to the mint so a
spec-declared registered estimator's floor passes recomputation +
equality, with provenance re-derived from authenticated members (never
read from admitted JSON, per D-133/ALT-D120). Full D-118 gauntlet
mandatory. Prerequisites: FCM-01 ALT-D120 round + full fresh delta land
clean. Consumption: packs re-spec back to the tighter estimator only if
this WO + both FCM items land before the freeze wave (D-133 cl.4).

DESIGN ADOPTED (2026-08-11 Sol xhigh consult, magistrate-adjudicated):
one spec-authoritative per-cell estimator dispatch (new shared
joulewise/floor_mint_estimator.py) consumed at ALL THREE mint sites —
postcollection equality, artifact construction, final evidence binding —
because patching steps 8-9 alone leaves a second default-core
recomputation and a default-only binder (the consult's named likely
failure mode). Pinned core, v2 pinset schema, report/artifact schemas,
and production specs all UNCHANGED this WO. Cost estimate 360-540
production LOC + 550-850 test LOC; three audit waves + D-121. Full
consult: session scratchpad mintvocab-consult-out.md (T4-late).
Implementation landed in #140 after the prerequisite estimator work.

## WO-SAMPLER-SUPERVISOR (registered 2026-08-11, T4; consult-adopted design)

Privileged-sampler lifecycle ownership for validate_powermetrics_fiducial:
a root-owned supervisor helper (single sudoers entry, no-argument invocation;
the current passwordless /usr/bin/powermetrics authorization MUST be removed
in the same migration or it bypasses the boundary) that owns spawn + signal +
reap of the actual sampler and applies kernel no-fork confinement before
exec. ADMISSION GATE (lead-owed, live Mac): real powermetrics must run with
process creation denied — if not, the design fails its gate and the honest
fallback is detect-and-refuse, never another identity-tracking formulation
(two failed: group-kill r0, identity preamble r1 — consult record custodied
T4). Until landed, the production script's census is detect-and-report only
and full ownership is documented UNSUPPORTED. Not on any critical path.

AMENDED (2026-08-11, round-3 audit FIND-1): the supervisor must also own
TEST-harness writer lifecycles — a killed test interpreter strands its
in-flight validate_powermetrics_fiducial.py writer (executed: interpreter
killed mid calibration_writer_bracket_rederive_conflict; writer survived
both zero-survivor guards from a fresh interpreter, unique witness root,
no ledger corruption). Required regression at closure: kill the test
interpreter mid-correction; a host census must come back empty via the
supervisor/parent-death watchdog (durable identity-checked registrations,
bounded SIGTERM->SIGKILL).

generated A5 row: kernel/checklist closure and the later D-079 acceptance/pin

  restart rows.

  non-selection context.

Authority: [D-078 clause 8 (confirmation round 9, registered limitation L1)](docs/decision_log.md). Acceptance: [FLOOR-BIND-01 acceptance](docs/process/state_kernel.json). Fence: Until this row closes, claim-bearing analysis may consume floor artifacts only from same-custody-session governed extraction; standalone artifacts are non-claim-bearing (D-078 clause 8 L1). Note: BLOCKED 2026-08-27 (T26 scope check; magistrate ruling Option 1 - sequence after _v4 mint closure, no pre-mint coupling). Row is PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED, not closed: the mint-side half of E1/E2 is discharged by S5 contract Q4/W6/W3 and D-117/D-120, but the claim-consumption half stands exactly as CR9-1 described it. At claim consumption the extraction report's bytes are never read (SHA only hex-syntax-checked, detection_floor.py:3472), admissible half-widths come from the artifact's own admissible_half_widths_j with _validate_estimate_math recomputing the corner from that same array (detection_floor.py:3106), and complete governed campaign membership is not enforced (inputs.py:1522 proves only that artifact-listed members occur in order, never the converse). E3 has no consumption-path regression at all. LEAD-EXECUTED PROOF that the reviewed repository pinset is inert against the artifact: six single-field mutations of mint1.json supplied through the explicit pinset route against the real frozen pack df-ph-decode-floor-mint1.json all returned an empty finding list, including operative_floor_six_decimal 7.377086 -> 9.999999 and extraction_spec_members 30 -> 999; _project_floor_mint_pinset_v2 validates those pins for pinset-internal consistency and then returns only family_identities and evidence_root_ids (detection_floor.py:2059, :2601). The cheap cure (compare the artifact's floor to the pinned six-decimal literal) was proposed by the lead and REFUTED by the design consult with a repro: scaling one component's widths by 0.99999999 moves the exact operative floor 7.377085735735073 -> 7.377085672752914, both rendering as 7.377086, and a non-maximizing width can change with no floor change at all - so floor-literal comparison can never discharge E2's 'any stored width mismatch'. LIVE REMAINDER: strengthened A + B per design-consult D6 - (A) widen the pinset projection to carry the v2 component-artifact digests and exact member dispositions and compare them against the artifact inside validate_floor_artifact; (B) accept per-root governed extraction-spec evidence at claim consumption, authenticate it against the v2 pin, replay floor_extraction, and compare replayed members/widths/corners/floors before populating bound_cell_ids. RESIDUAL (i) for a post-_v4 named decision: E1 is NOT discharged by the pinset route - E1 requires binding to the extraction report and source-member disposition, or rederivation, and a reviewed pin is a third anchor that is neither; consult verbatim: 'Claim consumption proves that the artifact matches reviewer-frozen assertions, but still cannot independently prove that those assertions match the governed extraction report and source bytes.' Closing on the pinset half alone would need an explicit E1 amendment. RESIDUAL (ii): B's --floor-extraction-spec hard-requirement for claim-bearing runs breaks four currently-passing claim fixtures (tests/test_analysis_integration.py:541, :1944, :3580, :3585) and must land with them re-fixtured with governed synthetic extraction specs and matching final-pinset pins in the same change. L1's workflow rule continues to mitigate until this closes. Record: docs/process_traces/2026-08-27-t26/floor-bind/ (00-README.md, p0-scopecheck.md, design-consult.md; D6 is the ready-to-execute implementation contract). Minted 2026-07-22 from confirmation round 9 (CR9-1, lead-reproduced). |

Authority: [D-078 clause 8 (confirmation round 9, registered limitation L1)](docs/decision_log.md). Acceptance: [FLOOR-BIND-01 acceptance](docs/process/state_kernel.json). Fence: Until this row closes, claim-bearing analysis may consume floor artifacts only from same-custody-session governed extraction; standalone artifacts are non-claim-bearing (D-078 clause 8 L1). Note: BLOCKED 2026-08-27 (T26 scope check; magistrate ruling Option 1 - sequence after _v4 mint closure, no pre-mint coupling). Row is PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED, not closed: the mint-side half of E1/E2 is discharged by S5 contract Q4/W6/W3 and D-117/D-120, but the claim-consumption half stands exactly as CR9-1 described it. At claim consumption the extraction report's bytes are never read (SHA only hex-syntax-checked, detection_floor.py:3472), admissible half-widths come from the artifact's own admissible_half_widths_j with _validate_estimate_math recomputing the corner from that same array (detection_floor.py:3106), and complete governed campaign membership is not enforced (inputs.py:1522 proves only that artifact-listed members occur in order, never the converse). E3 has no consumption-path regression at all. LEAD-EXECUTED PROOF that the reviewed repository pinset is inert against the artifact: six single-field mutations of mint1.json supplied through the explicit pinset route against the real frozen pack df-ph-decode-floor-mint1.json all returned an empty finding list, including operative_floor_six_decimal 7.377086 -> 9.999999 and extraction_spec_members 30 -> 999; _project_floor_mint_pinset_v2 validates those pins for pinset-internal consistency and then returns only family_identities and evidence_root_ids (detection_floor.py:2059, :2601). The cheap cure (compare the artifact's floor to the pinned six-decimal literal) was proposed by the lead and REFUTED by the design consult with a repro: scaling one component's widths by 0.99999999 moves the exact operative floor 7.377085735735073 -> 7.377085672752914, both rendering as 7.377086, and a non-maximizing width can change with no floor change at all - so floor-literal comparison can never discharge E2's 'any stored width mismatch'. LIVE REMAINDER: strengthened A + B per design-consult D6 - (A) widen the pinset projection to carry the v2 component-artifact digests and exact member dispositions and compare them against the artifact inside validate_floor_artifact; (B) accept per-root governed extraction-spec evidence at claim consumption, authenticate it against the v2 pin, replay floor_extraction, and compare replayed members/widths/corners/floors before populating bound_cell_ids. RESIDUAL (i) for a post-_v4 named decision: E1 is NOT discharged by the pinset route - E1 requires binding to the extraction report and source-member disposition, or rederivation, and a reviewed pin is a third anchor that is neither; consult verbatim: 'Claim consumption proves that the artifact matches reviewer-frozen assertions, but still cannot independently prove that those assertions match the governed extraction report and source bytes.' Closing on the pinset half alone would need an explicit E1 amendment. RESIDUAL (ii): B's --floor-extraction-spec hard-requirement for claim-bearing runs breaks four currently-passing claim fixtures (tests/test_analysis_integration.py:541, :1944, :3580, :3585) and must land with them re-fixtured with governed synthetic extraction specs and matching final-pinset pins in the same change. L1's workflow rule continues to mitigate until this closes. Record: docs/process_traces/2026-08-27-t26/floor-bind/ (00-README.md, p0-scopecheck.md, design-consult.md; D6 is the ready-to-execute implementation contract). Minted 2026-07-22 from confirmation round 9 (CR9-1, lead-reproduced). |

## FLAKE-CALEXITS-311-REDERIVE (registered 2026-08-12, T6; FIX APPLIED on impl/wo-detect-pulses-budget 5449e58, rides Phase 2)

New calexits hosted-flake signature distinct from the #139-killed class:
`test_parameterized_durable_public_cli_witnesses` FAILED on 3.11 only
(`AssertionError: 1 != 0 : correction=calibration_output_requires_rederive`,
run 31622634705 on PR #143 — a PR that does not touch the module) while the
same exclusive leg passed on #135/#141/#142 the same day. ESCALATED same day (second same-signature failure: PR #144, case
calibration_quiet_mac_auth_required, 18:31Z) -> Sol xhigh root-cause per the
standing trigger: REPRODUCED — the corrected public-writer witness runs a
40x accelerated test clock over a still REAL-TIME, scheduler-sensitive pulse
producer; a 120 ms writer suspension reproduces the exact hosted failure
shape (status=invalid, b_fiducial_s=null) on the valid-writer step. Harness
false negative; both failing runs certified safe-to-rerun (each failed case
passed on its automatic later in-job execution; neither PR touches the
writer). Fix applied on `impl/wo-detect-pulses-budget`: the test-only producer
now uses logical protocol-event time and waits for durable sampler
acknowledgements at each command transition, so command stamps, pulse
duration, and schedule advancement are scheduler-independent. The production
real-time driver and fail-closed detector remain unchanged; the
corrected-writer assertion now surfaces the detector subbranch, and a
producer-mid-pulse 120 ms delay regression proves identical evidence bytes.
This deliberately rides the Phase-2 merge because the branch already carries
stale authenticated estimator inputs; landing it separately on main would
require a second D-079 re-issuance. Diagnosis: T6 scratchpad
calexits-flake-diag.md (custody with T6 session record).
